# CLI-First v1 Delivery Plan

Alice should install the CLI, sign in through the small Ory account UI, configure mixed model providers, load documents, experiment, evaluate when useful and explicitly build/release. A colleague should be able to recreate supported configuration from Git without receiving Alice's secrets or production traffic state.

Use this guide to find the issues that implement a capability and the prerequisite contracts they need. **GitHub issue bodies are the engineering source of truth for implementation requirements and acceptance tests.** Topic guides explain how the product works. ADRs explain the architectural choices.

The [capability map](#decision-coverage) retains all 118 accepted requirement mappings, grouped by subject. It is an index, not a competing specification. Preserve the [implementation template](../../.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/implementation.md) and [Definition of Done](definition-of-done.md). If sources conflict, resolve the conflict explicitly before implementation.

| Reader's question | Start here |
| --- | --- |
| What belongs in v1? | [Delivery boundaries](#delivery-boundaries) |
| Which guide explains my area? | [Read by concern](#read-by-concern) |
| Which contracts must be specified first? | [Specification gates](#specification-gates) |
| Which tickets implement a capability? | [Capability map](#decision-coverage) |
| What was checked previously? | [Historical reconciliation evidence](#reconciliation-evidence) |

## Delivery Boundaries

**Current handoff check:** the [2026-10-04 authentication correction](cli-reconciliation-report.md#authentication-handoff-2026-10-04) records the backend, account-UI and CLI ownership updates and their verification. The [2026-10-03 reconciliation report](cli-reconciliation-report.md#handoff-recheck) retains the earlier Access, recovery and reference-cleanup evidence. Publish the maintained uncommitted documentation before handing a clean checkout to another developer. The eight specification tickets below are ordinary prerequisite engineering work, not reopened product decisions.

- **V1:** Rust CLI, native OpenAPI-generated application clients, required standalone Kratos/Hydra account UI and existing backend/Compose scope. No generator package is prescribed. Generated-client qualification starts against existing contracts and expands with feature delivery.
- **Deferred Studio:** #12, #13, #81 and #83-#94 remain open outside v1 and follow the same CLI-led product behavior. They cannot block v1 directly or transitively.
- **Preserved foundations:** Existing completed issues remain closed; existing Kratos/OIDC, Access, grant idempotency, native OpenAPI and backend tooling are not reimplemented by planning. Most RAG/CLI capabilities remain unimplemented.
- **Separate authority:** Editing, direct Pipeline querying, evaluation and configuration import do not publish. Underlying Build establishes readiness; selected policy may coordinate separately authorized conditional publication. Shared connection changes retain their own live-impact guards.
- **Interface-only extension:** #53 retains planned `PipelineHook`/`WebhookDispatcher` declarations. Executable hooks, subscriptions and webhook delivery remain excluded.
- **No hidden implementation:** Documentation and issue reconciliation ran no product tests, migrations, model calls, contract generation or dependency installation and created no commit.

## Issue Boundaries

#80 is reused for generator-neutral client integration under the CLI epic. #82 is reused for the standalone account UI under Access, without a Studio dependency. #101 uses CLI/account-UI qualification instead of #94 while retaining upgrade, MCP, capacity and backend safety gates.

#61 becomes a coordinator for automatic admission, existing-target conditional publication, atomic first-Deployment binding and their authorization/crash/race qualification. #63 similarly coordinates independently guarded candidate attachment, allocation, rejection, promotion and rollback. #66 retains case revision/review ownership; benchmark freezing, starter generation, individual execution and dataset operations have focused tickets. #44 retains bounded source sync; configuration and authentication are separate prerequisites. Epics group delivery, not implementation detail; the configuration epic creates no new backend domain or provisioning engine.

Other existing tickets retain coherent boundaries where splitting would merely create coding chores. For example, atomic provider-secret replacement and the gateway's dispatch checks belong to their own complete operations; DTOs, routes and tables are tasks within those operations, not separate tickets.

### Authentication handoff

Alice's browser callback returns, but that alone does not mean her CLI login is ready. The account UI must complete Ory's required steps, the backend must verify her current identity and admission, and the CLI must save the validated credentials safely. Each step has an owner:

| Work | Owning ticket and boundary |
| --- | --- |
| Specify credential dispatch and permission contracts | [Access contracts](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/116) defines the compatible session extension, sole verifier for each credential family, cookie CSRF, current checks and exact capability mappings. |
| Verify human bearer requests | [Hydra integration](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/117) extends the session operation and protected routes. Every request checks Hydra, current Kratos eligibility and the local human; no positive authentication cache substitutes for those checks. |
| Manage application accounts and keys | [Application lifecycle](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/30) owns creation, full-authority issuance, safe metadata, independent revocation and terminal retirement. [Ordinary administration](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/28) owns membership/group/grant changes and handover. |
| Render the required browser journey | [Account UI](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/82) handles Ory registration, login, password/settings, configured MFA, recovery and consent states. It neither issues tokens nor decides recovery or admission policy. |
| Activate an invitation | [Invitations](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/119) depends on both controlled bootstrap/admission and ordinary administration. It uses #28's current checks rather than writing permissions through another path. |
| Present human login and application connection | [Human CLI login](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/160) owns protected local storage/refresh/logout; [application connection](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/161) invokes #30/#28. Neither client can replace backend checks. |
| Qualify the complete result | [Security qualification](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/96) now explicitly depends on the real CLI login. [First-use qualification](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/185) exercises the required screens, backend admission and saved-login outcome with shipped artifacts. |

[Suspension and restoration](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/188) and [scoped operator recovery](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/189) keep their existing ownership. Partial Kratos/Hydra cleanup never lifts a local suspension. Closed [Kratos foundation work](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/25) remains completed; its original exclusion of Hydra does not exclude the later integration or required standalone UI.

## Specification Gates

These tickets settle concrete contracts already identified as incomplete by the accepted design. They are not invitations to reopen settled UX or fabricate permission identifiers. Dependent implementation remains blocked until reviewed contracts exist. Numeric values not selected by the architecture require measured qualification, not guessed guarantees.

For example, the application-key prefix is already settled: `ifm_app_`, with Ory formats unchanged. Engineers still need to specify the complete verification and dispatch contract. Similarly, project-budget changes are human-only, while permitted humans and applications may inspect budgets. The exact permissions, targets, schemas and revision checks still belong to the Access, model and portability contract tickets below. Choosing the rule does not implement or test it.

The same distinction applies to tracing. Humans and applications may receive inspection permission. Only authorized humans may change project recording or retention, and explicit history deletion needs a separate permission. Query and query-inspection permissions name exact Pipelines or Deployments. There is no Experiment resource. Automatic expiry and source cleanup remain ordinary backend workflows.

The Access and tracing contracts still need exact mappings, non-query scopes, initial grants, transitions, schemas and limits. The portability contract must represent these rules during import. Runtime qualification remains separate work.

<!-- BEGIN GENERATED GATES -->

**Models and portable settings.** Metadata suggestions must identify their source, remain editable and stay separate from credentials. The model and portability contracts still need field ownership, interpretation of metadata sources, bounds, authentication mappings and portable schemas. [Model qualification](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/37), [recreation tests](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/155) and [first-use tests](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/185) must verify real behavior. Documentation and mock screens are not that evidence.

**Progress and change reviews.** Reuse existing job and evaluation results. Show answering, scoring and incomplete cases honestly, with current access checks and safe continuation. The job, Evaluation and automation contracts must define their precise representations. These eight contracts remain prerequisites, but work whose own prerequisites are ready can proceed. The UX choices for Pipeline Query/Build, Evaluation, tracing, import and automation are settled. Specifying fields must not reopen those product choices.

The [automation contract](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/156) specifies terminal, plain-text and JSON change displays. [Shared CLI behavior](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/158) implements them and [CLI qualification](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/187) tests them. The [Pipeline contract](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/128) retains exact admission and revision checks. Colors do not grant publication permission or change the Build rules.

**Evaluation.** Freeze tested inputs, keep reference answers out of Pipeline execution and recognize complete mapped passage coverage even when chunk boundaries change. Distinguish missing execution dependencies from unscorable metrics. Keep the original case selection protected rather than silently reducing it. The [Evaluation contract](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/140) still needs exact selectors, coordinates, failure representations and permitted result views. [Evaluation qualification](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/71) and CLI qualification require runtime evidence.

**Tracing.** Diagnostic failures do not fail an otherwise successful query. Opt-out, re-enable and deletion behavior is settled. The [tracing contract](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/147) still needs exact ordering of capture, persistence and delivery, bounded storage and access-safe outcomes. The automation contract maps those outcomes to CLI output. [Tracing qualification](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/150) and CLI qualification must test failures and races without weakening mandatory business checks or trace permissions.

**Import.** Reject a known invalid or outdated review before changing resources. If a later race stops an import, keep completed work. Continuing the original inputs is different from approving a new import. The [portability contract](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/151) still needs equivalence rules, schemas and coordination of preconditions with child-operation outcomes. The job and automation contracts own recovery and output. Recreation and CLI qualification must test races and the absence of hidden execution. Import adds no whole-project transaction or automatic rollback.

**Automation.** Accepting work, inspecting it and waiting for its completion have different success meanings. An uncertain submission must remain uncertain. The automation contract still needs reviewed schemas, per-domain mappings, signal handling and output compatibility. The [job contract](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/124) owns authoritative lookup and continuation. Shared CLI behavior and [job presentation](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/175) implement these rules. CLI qualification tests actual effects, exit codes and output channels. A completed UX workshop does not close these engineering tickets or unblock every dependent implementation.

| Issue | Contract to settle |
| --- | --- |
| [#116](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/116) | Specify CLI identity dispatch, authority and review contracts |
| [#121](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/121) | Specify model metadata, connection impact and embedding compatibility contracts |
| [#124](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/124) | Specify admission lookup and durable progress/recovery contracts |
| [#128](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/128) | Specify Pipeline Query and policy-controlled Build contracts |
| [#140](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/140) | Specify generated-case, passage and evaluation admission contracts |
| [#147](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/147) | Specify independent platform and RAG tracing policy contracts |
| [#151](https://github.com/matejpalenik/inframeld/issues/151) | Specify complete TOML resources and reviewed import/export contracts |

## Capability Map

Each named capability lists its owners, the combined direct prerequisites of those owners and the tickets responsible for qualification. Some owners are future Studio tickets because Studio must follow the same product rules. Their prerequisites are not automatically v1 blockers. The exact blocking relationships remain in the individual GitHub tickets and native dependency graph.

Qualification links identify **planned evidence**, not tests executed during reconciliation. The earlier proposal to export live Deployment configuration is explicitly superseded. Current work covers configuration-only export and separately requested evaluation datasets.

| Area | Capabilities |
| --- | --- |
| Release scope | [Delivery scope](#delivery-scope) |
| Identity and resource selection | [Targets, identity and permissions](#targets-identity-and-permissions) |
| Local services | [Runtime and diagnosis](#local-runtime-and-diagnosis) |
| First use and model access | [Setup, models and prepared data](#setup-models-and-prepared-data) |
| Development loop | [Pipelines, releases and feedback](#pipeline-development-releases-and-feedback) |
| Tests and datasets | [Evaluation](#evaluation-and-portable-datasets) |
| Diagnostics | [Tracing and retention](#tracing-and-retention) |
| Environment recreation | [Configuration files](#configuration-files-and-recreation) |
| Scripts and durable work | [Automation, progress and recovery](#automation-progress-and-recovery) |

## Issue Inventory

Implementation tickets contain starting point, worked context, owner/boundaries, ordered workflow, state, input/success/failures, recovery, security/bounds and behavioral tests. Specification and qualification tickets adapt that structure. Epics are concise maps. Every new v1 ticket uses the existing milestone; closed foundations keep their state and history.

## Qualification Still Required

No runtime evidence is created by this reconciliation. Required gates include real Ory browser/device/refresh behavior and protected storage; generated-client compatibility; gateway-only routing, bounded retries and telemetry suppression; source-coordinate proof; model pricing/token bounds and concurrent budget accounting; parser/vector safety and measured capacity; protected dataset import/reimport; release races; backup/restore/upgrade; and complete CLI human/noninteractive/dry-run/signal/recovery journeys.

Preserve actual limitations: a configuration export does not reproduce old provider aliases/vectors; a receipt cannot replay the lost answer; canceled provider work may still be charged; an unrecorded/erased trace cannot be recovered; downloaded dataset copies cannot be recalled. Sample packaging still needs provenance/license review and asset/runtime pins before distribution. Documentation checks certify none of these runtime behaviors.
